- 2026-10-01: the owner: entering any building, or a tour taking you in, "you are blinded by the walls… even though you are inside". Measured on the densities' walk-through: arriving in the lobby the wanderer stood 10.5 m from its centre and the camera 7 m behind, at 17.4 m, outside the 13 m round wall, looking back in through the entrance's sheet of night. Two fixes:
  - In every place apart (monuments' lobbies and rooms, the pyramid, the deep archive) the camera keeps in front of any solid surface between it and the wanderer, as it already did in the temple (`apartBlockers` in `main.ts`: opaque, non-skinned meshes near you, not sky shells or points, gathered once a second; `FollowCamera` casts its ray ten times a second).
  - Every building's stone (`landStone`, the one material of all the architecture) thins away in a fine dither where it stands near the line from the camera to the wanderer's chest (`seeThrough` in `stoneworks.ts`): upright faces only, never the floor, nothing beyond the wanderer. Outside too: a monument's wall between you and the camera opens a window.
- 2026-10-06: the end-to-end tours fixed (v4 item 1). What broke them: between rooms the walk sent the wanderer to the door onward but gave up after 9 s and stepped into the next room through the dark. The doors stand 20–60 m from where a room begins, and walking there by set course (which runs only beyond ~28 m and slows as it nears) takes 9–16 s, so every room but the Past Choices' small ones was left by a fade mid-walk instead of its door: the tour "went through rooms without going through them". The planning note's guesses (autofly arrival epsilon, a segment watchdog, capsule tug-of-war in `templeTour.ts`/`autofly.ts`) don't match the code: neither file takes part in the walk-throughs (`walkFrame` in `main.ts`). Now:
  - The tour runs to the door at full run speed (the same input as its flights) and goes through it on foot; it steps in through the dark only if the way is truly held up (no nearer for 4 s, or 40 s in all).
  - On the way between monuments the same check (no nearer for 6 s) replaces waiting out a 150 s limit while held up before a door ("it gets stuck").
  - Leaving one monument for the next, the walk now waits until it is out ("leaving"). It used to keep the room's last step meanwhile, and the next frame took the half-left monument for the next one and moved on again: the next monument's lobby was skipped and the flight there was lost to the dark.
  - A walk to a door that stalls (the Past Choices' pool lies across the straight way from the lobby's start to its door) steps round to one side and goes on.
  - Skip pressed while a door was already taking you on no longer crosses into the same room a second time; Skip on the way somewhere is answered on arrival, never carried into the next room.
  - Background voices give way while a tour runs (one speaking stops when the tour begins; none starts until it ends).
  - Verified headless with every frame held at 1/60 s: the densities' nine stops and the Past Choices' seated rooms each crossed at their doors, and the grand tour's hand-off from the densities to the school of the adept (out, the flight, its lobby walked). WebGPU and forced WebGL2 boot clean.
- 2026-10-07: collision, the whole pass (v4 item 2; the owner: the wanderer walked through the densities' fountain "and other things too"). The F2 note's `buildFountain()`/`collideCapsule()` don't exist; the real gap: the controller only collides with `colliders` (`terrain.ts`), and inside the monuments nothing was ever registered there (only each room's outer `confine`), so every prop in every room was walk-through. Now:
  - Colliders can be turned boxes as well as circles (`Collider.hx/hz/ang`, `pushOut` in `terrain.ts`, used by the controller on land, swimming and on the lake floor; two passes, so out of one is never into its neighbour).
  - Rooms declare what stands solid: a stage's `solids` (static, in `monument.ts`) or a room's `solids()` (rooms laid out by seed); the journey makes them colliders while the room stands and frees them with it. Rule: anything new and solid in a room goes in its solids.
  - The densities: the lobby's fountain (basin and lip) and its eight steles; Room 1's basalt towers, boulders and the living stone; Room 2's trunks and the arch's two trees; Room 3's dais, its obelisks and the people standing and kneeling; Room 4's towers, every house and the people standing or sitting; Room 5's magician.
  - The school of the adept: the lobby's quartz plinth and eight columns; the crucible's bowl (its hearth stays a step you stand on); the radiance's basin and door jambs; the stones' great plinth and four small plinths; the disciplines' walls, piers, table, sink and its people; the others' boulders and the ring of sitters.
  - Past choices: the lobby's twelve columns (its pool was already held by `confine`).
  - The Duat: its six gates' posts and the stone lamps along the way (registered while the Duat stands).
  - The world: the Egypt gate's jambs; the palms near you (streamed with them); the caves' crystals; the underwater ruins (every column, drum, block and shaft resting on the floor, from its own placement: the controller's comment said they held you out, but nothing was registered); the tree station's and the tree of life's trunks.
  - Already solid and left as they were: the temple (columns, plinths, signs, braziers, altar, the Choice, the ruins), the monuments' exteriors, homes' standing stones, trees, rocks and crystal clusters, groves.
  - Not solid on purpose: figures who walk or run (they would push you about), threads and figures of light in the lessons, seats (you walk onto them), hearths and platforms you stand on, the coffer in the King's Chamber (its rite is lying in it).
  - The tours step round what now stands in their way (the A1 detour), e.g. the fountain between the densities lobby's start and its door, Room 5's magician on the way to the door.
  - Video 1's clips (a waterfall basin, glowing flora on a slope): there is no waterfall in the code; the nearest match is a cave's back wall and crystals (now solid). The owner to confirm where it was filmed.
- 2026-10-07: the Duat is dry ground (v4 item 3; the owner: it read as "swimming over dry ground"). The cause was one number: the Duat is built at `DUAT_ORIGIN` = the pyramid's origin (y 14) + (−140, −30, 60), so its sand lay at y ≈ −16, below the world's water line (`WATER_Y` 0). The controller swims wherever the ground is below the water, so in the Duat the wanderer became the orb and swam, the round button's word read "Dive", the first-time tip said "Tap the round button to dive…", and the underwater look came on. The F3 note's guesses (`checkWater()`, `fluidBody` breaststroke, a "Dive" label in `tourBar.ts`) don't match the code; nothing needed gating: everything followed `player.swimming`.
  - The Duat now stands at y 10 (offset −4; its lowest dune is ~2.4 m under its origin), so all its ground is above the water: walking, no orb, no "Dive", no dive tip, no underwater tint. Nothing else in it changed.
  - Hour 1, "The waters of Nun", is renamed "The First Mound (Vision of Nun)" (the batch's proposal; awaiting the owner's word). Its vision of light (the waters, the mound, the barque) stays: a vision, not the place.
  - Narration (held: the recordings are pending from the owner's pipeline; nothing invented): `DUAT_TRACKS` in `duat.ts` is the wiring point (entry, six hours, dawn). The entry plays on coming in, each hour's as you first come to it, the dawn's on the stair; all null (silent) until the files arrive. (The entry was filled 2026-10-07: see "the Duat speaks".)
- 2026-10-07: the Duat's pacing, textures and finale (v4 item 6, after the dry-ground fix).
  - Dead holds: each form of an hour held 9 s plus a 4.5 s turn (a still sun orb on screen ~13–18 s); now 6 s and 3.5 s (`HOLD`, `keysFor` in `duat.ts`), so an hour of four forms tells in ~24 s instead of ~36. Between hours the tour runs (the same input as the walk-throughs), so the sand between stories is crossed in seconds, not walked for ten.
  - The frozen "Dawn": the stair's 22 m was climbed at walking pace (~14 s) with the camera left wherever the last story had turned it, pitched at the stair's corner, before the dawn exit (within 4 m of the stair's top) fired. Now the climb runs, and the view turns to the dawn the stair rises into (the Duat's sky glows low toward local (−0.3, −0.9); `gravityPoint` in `main.ts`). At the top the exit takes you out onto the apex and the tour ends ("The walk is complete"), giving the controls back. The camera is levelled coming into the Duat (it could arrive still looking down into the pit).
  - Muddy dunes: the sand's normal map was read through the ground plane's own UVs, one tile stretched over the whole 112 m. The sand is now read in the Duat's own frame (its world position is ~50 km out): the scan at two scales, a fine third layer near, its occlusion, relief at the same scales, and broad drifts of lighter and darker sand. The gorge's wall has 44 rows instead of 14 (its 2 m facets read as flat, blurred stone).
  - Reeds through the steps: the last hour (the Field of Reeds) stood on the stair, and its reeds were laid at the stair's height over its blocks. It is now told 6.5 m beside the stair on the sand, outward from the river; the tour turns to wherever each story actually stands (`Duat.hours()` heading).
  - The stray black sphere: the weighing's heart was an opaque sphere whose colour was multiplied by its fading light, so before it lit and after it rose away it hung in the air as a black ball. It is now light (added to what is behind it) and hidden when dark.
  - Still frames of the Duat (`?shot=duat-k`) stand under the hour's gate: the brown band at the top of those frames is the gate's lintel overhead, not a ceiling.
- 2026-10-07: the Duat speaks (v4 item 3, audio half; the owner's recording from PR #196, used as delivered: no re-encoding or re-timing). `DUAT` in `content/narration.json` ("The Duat", Aria, `audio/duat/duat-narration.mp3`, duration 278.56 s, the decoded length, as tracks played by progress need; the 41 cues embedded as the other tracks carry theirs; trigger "duat-landfall"). It plays through `DUAT_TRACKS.entry` in `duat.ts`, from `enterDuatCrossing` in `main.ts`: once each time you come into the Duat (by the pit's hidden door or its tour), and carries on as you walk the hours; it isn't cut on leaving. The hours' and the dawn's own slots stay empty. No subtitles show (the game has none since 2026-09-26); the cues are in the catalogue for alignment. The owner merges `narration/*` audio PRs; the game's own PRs are merged as they go (the owner, 2026-10-07: "merge as you go always").
- 2026-10-07: video 2's defects (v4 item 5). The video isn't in the repo, and the F5 note's campfire sequence, `REST_HOLD_DURATION`, character LOD and cape cloth simulation don't exist in the code. The scene matching its description (a seated hold by a fire with embers, an orbiting view, a stand-up) is the garden lesson, "The Fire in the Hand" (L05).
  - The dead first half-minute: the coal burned still in the palm from 0 s. The opening now follows its words (`garden.ts`): the coal waits dim among the hearth's stones; at "a coal, lifted from a fire" (11.5–16 s) it rises in an arc into the palm; its heat gathers to "burning in your palm" (26 s) and flares once at "this is how anger comes" (30 s); the hand brightens as "look at your hands" is spoken. The rest of the timeline is unchanged.
  - Embers through the character: the fire's sparks, smoke and embers now keep out of the line from the camera to the wanderer and melt near the lens (`outOfTheWay`, the house rule for effects). Only the garden's: the shared lesson `cloud()` also builds Room 0's finale, which gathers at your own heart.
  - The "cape" on standing up: the wanderer's three ribbons of light trail up to 1.4 m from the hands and the crown, drawn through everything (additive, no depth); as the body rose from sitting they lagged and swept through the legs and torso. They now rest while seated and through the stand-up (`k.sit`), and start afresh.
  - The "patchy, low-detail" character: the wanderer has no LOD and no textures to fail; the likeliest cause is contemplation's fade of the body (after 15 s still). Not changed without seeing the video; for the owner to confirm.
- 2026-10-07: Phase 3, S5, the wanderer grounded (`player/wanderer.ts` contact shadow; main.ts sets `groundY` and `groundNormal` from `standAt`). Before/after: `docs/sweeps/s5-wanderer/`.
  - A soft contact shadow under the feet: darkness, not light (the owner removed its pool of light, which chased it over the floor), so the body stands on the ground rather than over it. A gaussian disc a little longer than wide, laid on the ground's slope; it stays on the ground when the body leaves it and softens and fades with the height (gone by ~2.4 m); none in the water, in flight, or as the body fades (contemplation, the temple tour, the seventh room).
  - The body itself was elevated with the shared glass (S3); its timid light is unchanged.
  - Stills of the wanderer formed: `?shot=breath&t=0.6&warm=3&webgl` (the one-frame stills catch it before its body forms; the WebGPU path loses the software GPU on a warm-up).

- 2026-10-08: offline play checked and made lighter on data (the owner: "does the download actually work… airplane mode… reduce data usage?"). It did work (tested: download, then with the network cut the game boots, narrations play, archive recordings play and seek). Three faults, fixed (`core/offline.ts`, `public/sw.js`, `generate-assets.js`):
  - Every new version re-downloaded everything (~357 MB): the cache was named after the build and wiped on update, with a confirm() pop-up. Now one cache survives updates; `assets.json` (made at prebuild) lists each file with a content hash and size, and a download fetches only new or changed files, and lets go of removed ones. Files kept under the old per-build caches are moved over once, not fetched again. Tested: a changed file in a new version fetched just that file (28 KB).
  - Playing on line kept nothing: now the recordings, models and textures you meet are kept as you go (whole files only), so they are never fetched twice, downloaded or not; the download then skips them.
  - iPhone: Safari's audio element asks for pieces of a file (Range); the worker answered with the whole file, which Safari refuses. Kept files now answer with exactly the piece (206, sliced from a blob, never read whole).
  - The ⋮ button says what it costs: "Download for offline play (357 MB)", "Update offline play (28 KB)" or "Offline play: ready ✓"; when an update is waiting a quiet word says so once. `?fresh=` asks the server itself past the worker's cache (the download, the file list).
- 2026-10-08: the owner (iPhone screenshots, upright and on its side): no ✎ to be seen; the welcome's text too big; "everything overlaps".
  - The ✎: a Home Screen app keeps its own storage, apart from Safari's, so `?dev=1` opened in Safari never reached it. The readings (tap ⋮ five times) now carry a switch, "✎ Turn on feedback mode", which sets it from inside the app and reloads.
  - The breath's overlay keeps clear of the wanderer at the centre of the view: the lungs, word and count hang at the top under the status bar (lungs 84–124 px), and the first-time explanation sits low, above the controls. On a phone on its side the lungs stand beside the word in one low row at the top, and the explanation sits at the foot.
  - The welcome is smaller and sized by the screen's height too (`min(vw, vh)`), so on a phone on its side the title, the owner's words, the line on how to move and "Touch the water to begin" all fit.
- 2026-10-07: dynamic graphics quality, tuned by config (v5 item 2). The adaptive controller (`core/quality.ts`) already scaled resolution first and rested effects after; its tuning is now in `public/quality.json`, read at boot over built-in defaults field by field (a missing or malformed field keeps its default; no file, all defaults), so retuning needs no code change: frame targets and hysteresis (`slowFps` 50, `goodFps` 56, 3 slow / 10 good windows of ~1 s, a slow tier retried after 30), the render-scale band and step (0.5–1.0 by 0.1), the sharpness floors (2× on a phone, kept from Samuel's "maximum resolution"; 1× on desktop), Low Power Mode's 30 fps cap, what counts as a lower-end device (≤ 4 cores or ≤ 3 GB, where the browser says), the start tier per kind of device, and the five tiers themselves (phone values given apart).
  - Proposed values (the defaults): a phone starts on "high" (the same picture as before: on a phone "full" differs only by a few more motes), a lower-end phone on "medium", a desktop on "full", a lower-end desktop on "high"; each climbs back when frames allow. On desktop the scale now uses the whole 0.5–1.0 band before any effect rests (it stopped at 0.7); on a phone the 2× floor still comes first, so sharpness on the iPhone is unchanged.
  - Automatic only, no settings UI (the owner leans that way; open decision). `AdaptiveQuality.override(name | null)` is the hook for an Auto / Low / Medium / High control if one is ever wanted.
  - Measure on the physical iPhone (directive 10); `#stats` shows the tier, scale and the last decision ("config" once the file is read).
- 2026-10-07: Phase 3, S3, the shared materials. Before/after sheets: `docs/sweeps/s3-materials/`.
  - The sky's sheen on stone (`gpu/tsl.ts` `skySheen`, emissive, never a mirror, never on a floor): an upright face turning away from you takes on the colour of the air, and where the moon or the low sun is behind it its edges catch a rim of that light (Sky's rim light), so stone stands out of the night by its silhouette. On all architecture (`landStone`), the pyramid's casing and the world's rocks; not on the ground (the owner: no glare chasing the floor) nor inside (`interior` stone takes none). Faces seen from behind (a gorge drawn inward) count by `abs(dot)`.
  - The glass-light body (`player/lightBody.ts`; the wanderer, the beings, the rooms' figures, the creatures): its edge is softer (a broad gathering toward the outline under a fine line, not a drawn edge), and where the moon or the low sun is behind it, its light comes through the body, most through the thin parts (translucency, in the colour of that light). The timid light within is unchanged.
  - Texture pipeline: the ground's and the temple's scans are sampled with anisotropy 16 (was 4 and 8), so their grain stays sharp at the grazing angles a phone held upright mostly sees.

- 2026-10-08: the graphics level can be chosen (the owner asked for a setting; supersedes "automatic only, no settings UI" in the dynamic-quality entry). ⋮ → Graphics: Automatic (recommended, the default: the game chooses as it goes, as before), Best, High, Medium, Light, Lowest (saves battery). A chosen level is held whatever the frame rate (`AdaptiveQuality.override`); remembered on the device (`inward-journey:graphics`); Automatic hands it back.
- 2026-10-07: a welcome at first launch (v5 item 4). Under the title, the first time only, the owner's words fade in over the world as it finishes loading ("Inward Journey is not a game to win. It is a place to wander, across still water, through seven gates, toward the light. There is nothing to collect and nowhere to be. Keep it open beside you while you work, or lie down and let it hold you. Move gently. Listen. Breathe."; his draft, lightly shaped, his to change), then one line of how to move (phone: the left thumb moves, a drag looks, a tap on what glows lets it speak; desktop: W A S D or the arrows, drag, click), then "Touch the water to begin", still the one way in (a touch anywhere). Entering remembers it (`inward-journey:welcomed`); later boots go straight to the water and the title. ⋮ → Welcome shows it again. Loading isn't held (it rises as the loading lifts) and sound starts on the touch, as before. Reduced motion shows it at once. This sits beside the "always the water first" rule: the water and its one touch are still the first and only step; the words are over it, not a page before it.
- 2026-10-07: Phase 3, S4, one lighting rig for every room's key light (`gpu/lightRig.ts` `keyShadow`). Before/after: `docs/sweeps/s4-lighting/`.
  - Rooms whose key light cast shadows had each made them its own way, most with three's default (a box 10 m across at 512 px), so a room's sun shadowed only near its middle, in blocks (Mars, the adept's pyramids and radiance, Atlantis; Egypt had the box but not the map). Now every key casts as the world's moon does: the box fitted to the place, the moon's map size for the quality tier (`setShadowSize`, from main.ts with the moon's), the same bias, normal bias and softness. Maldek's terrace sun casts too.
  - Each room keeps its own light's colour, strength and direction; only how its shadow is made is shared. A new room's key light takes `keyShadow(light, radius)`.

- 2026-10-08: the owner: "for start the tour, don't use an emoji ▶️, make it beautiful and on theme"; the breathing "should trigger the animation and the instructions for breathing on screen… more natural and pretty, with a lungs animation in blueish color".
  - The tour's offer (`#tour-start`) is a dark pill with a gold hairline: the seed of life drawn in thin gold, slowly turning and breathing, then "Begin the tour" in italic gold over the place's name. No emoji.
  - The breath's overlay (`#breath-cue`) shows the moment the breath begins ("Breathe with me" until the first count), not only once counting. Over the words, a pair of lungs made of particles (the owner: "use the particles… expanding, holding and deflating"; `ui/breathLungs.ts`, a canvas of ~1,100 small blue lights: the outline denser, the inside thinner, the airway and bronchi fine, each light drifting and flickering on its own) moves with the breath itself (`breath.open`, the same eased value as the ring of light): it swells outward and down from the airway and brightens on the inhale, shimmers on the hold, and deflates on the release; motes travel down the windpipe into the lungs on the inhale and rise out and away on the release. Words: Breathe in, Hold, Release, Rest. Reduced motion holds the lungs still, half full.

  - Proposed per density (the owner to judge): 0 the beginning, near (the light gathers at your heart); 1 wide (centre in the middle distance toward the volcano: the stone of sparks in front, the tree-door, the sea stacks, the volcano behind); 2 near (pulled back, the camera stood inside a tree behind the start); 3 near (the seam between the two stages); 4 wide (the village round the plaza and all the threads); 5 near (the magician); 6 near (the black hole); 7 near (the core).
  - Room 1's volcano: a ribbed, gullied silhouette (buttresses of old flows, a broken crater rim lower on one side), rock with strata and crust, rivers of fire flickering with heated rock glowing either side, and a faint apron of cooling fire where they reach the plain.

Carved in thin gold light, as the Duat's gates carry their winged suns (`atonSegments` in `past/egypt.ts`, ribbons): a disk of two rings with the uraeus hanging from it, thirteen straight rays fanning down, each ending in a small open hand, two holding out an ankh. Great on the pyramid's north face above the door (laid on the plane of the courses' edges, drawn a little taller so the disk reads round from below; its rays reach down toward the door), and on the faces of the valley temple's two pylons. It turns aside from water ahead and steps round anything that holds it up. It drives the controller with a stick as a thumb would, so the colliders hold it and it never passes through anything.

  - The words are on the audio clock with the ring, so they never drift from it. Aria's voice slot is unchanged (still pending).

- 2026-10-07: the world after the veil (v5 item 6; the owner's brief `prompts/opus-densities-area-prompt.md`; audio `public/audio/densities-area/densities-area-cine-1…7.mp3`, Aria, used as they are).
  - The one door (`scenes/afterVeil/door.ts`, site `VEIL_HALL` in `terrain.ts`, toward the deep night):
    - a tall door of old dark stone in courses, a heavy lintel, its seams fine lines of light, a faint pale light in its opening;
    - round it a ring of the world's own trees under dark crowns, spores of light low;
    - the angel waits beside it, and coming within ~11 m it turns to you and speaks beat 1 (the choice), once a visit (not again as you come back out through the door).
    - Through the door, a white fade (`Journey.white`, `#fade.white`), not dark. On the map as "The world after the veil".
  - Within (`scenes/afterVeil/area.ts`, a journey of one stage at x = 22000, lifted 8 m so the vale stays above the water line): one path, a corridor about it (`veilConfine`), no more doors. Beats 2–7 begin as you reach their places, in order, each once.
    - 2, the forest walk: dense still trees, light in the grain (the world's bark), under dark crowns that thin as you go. Ground mist melts as you reach it and thins with progress. The air goes from heavy amber to white-gold.
    - 3, the clearing: warm air, ~2,600 drifting motes, the ground lit with small lights like a meadow, soft shafts falling into it, and fine threads of light joining still motes (no veil between minds).
    - 4, the newcomers' ground: five figures of dim cold glass light at the treeline, sitting or standing guard, angular dark shards of their old armour drifting slowly about them. A bent spear of shadow and a cracked shield lie half sunk. They never approach.
    - 5, the war in heaven, weather not war: soft columns of warm light standing on one side (the guardians). On the other, tall slender glass-light forms in cold light, a little crooked, their idle playing slowly backward (beautiful but wrong), each with a slow spiral drawing motes in and dimming them. Arcs of thought cross between, pulses running along them. The path between is untouched.
    - 6, the mirror: from the height, the old world turning, out over the abyss and a little below the eye: seas, coasts, land in two scales, ice, weather, a glint, its rim of air. Threads, gold and shadow, come down toward it and fade before they touch; the shadow ones dissolve wherever the gaze falls straight on them.
    - 7, the laying down: a glade with a ring of twelve swords of light on the ground, crumbling and dimming. After its line the angel gestures to the way home and comes apart into motes that drift after you and fade. The path leads on out (white fade) to the door.
  - The angel (`scenes/afterVeil/angel.ts`, recipe `ANGEL` in `world/figures.ts`): the game's particle character on the wanderer's skeleton, warm gold light-dust, a veil and a long robe, no wings. It walks the recorded walk a little ahead of you (≤1.15 m/s, gliding on faster only if left far behind), stops at its next place until you come, turns to you while it speaks, and goes on when the line ends. A pause holds it.
  - The view frames what the angel speaks of (the room's own gravity point, `Room.centre`): the angel, the clearing, the newcomers, the vale, the old world, the glade.
  - Far scenes rest until you come near (`windows`), so their lines never cross another place's sky.
  - Stills: `?shot=veil-door`, `veil-<2..7>`.
  - Flagged, beyond the brief (the owner to judge):
    - the door's site and its own ring of trees;
    - beat 1 spoken out in the world at the door;
    - no camera float in the clearing;
    - the husks' backward idle;
    - the angel gliding to catch up;
    - the way out is the path's end fading white;
    - not yet in Tours.
- 2026-10-07: the long descent (v5 item 7; the owner's brief `prompts/opus-wanderer-area-prompt.md` and its treatment `opus-wanderer-visual-design.md`; audio `public/audio/wanderer-area/wanderer-cine-1…7.mp3`, Aria, used as delivered). The wanderer's own story, walked in the clouds high above the world.
  - The way in (`scenes/wanderer/portal.ts`, site `WANDERER_HALL` in `terrain.ts`): one shaft of white-gold light, breathing slowly, rising high from a meadow (seen from far away), its pool of light on the ground, motes rising up it, out of a broken ring of etched stones (two fallen). Walking into it: a white fade. On the map as "The long descent".
  - Within (`scenes/wanderer/area.ts`, a journey of one stage at x = 22000, its way 12–40 m up, above the water line): one path over a sea of cloud, a corridor about it (`wandererConfine`), no more gates. Beats 1–7 begin as you reach their places, in order, each once:
    - 1 the council: twelve pale beams leaning in, three gold rings turning overhead, a warm gold ground, threads joining them;
    - 2 the descent: a ramp spiralling down past turning crystals and lamps whose light runs along them in a wave, then thinning;
    - 3 the veil: thick grey air, memories drifting past and dissolving, your light dimming; the guide stops at the far edge and doesn't follow;
    - 4 the life: rain, dark earth, one streetlamp, small warm memories that fade as you come to them, dark houses far off;
    - 5 the ache: a long road under the deepest sky (aurora), one warm region too much like home; the guide walks beside you again;
    - 6 the remembering: dawn, a book of light, a pale stranger, a moment when everything stops, the still pool holding the sky and your reflection in gold;
    - 7 the radiating: an overlook above a sleeping city (its lights on dark land risen above the cloud sea), rings of warm light going out from you toward it, the council's ripples far off. The way goes on out (white fade) to the shaft.
  - The arc is the game's own moods, forced along the way (`moodForce` in `world/moods.ts`, eased as ever): golden, twilight, dusk draining grey, night, the deep, dawn, sunrise. The place keeps the world's real sky and clouds (`journeyHost(hall, true)`), and the moods run while you are in it.
  - The guide is the angel of the world after the veil (`scenes/afterVeil/angel.ts`), here the wanderer's own higher self, closer by, beside you in 5–7.
  - Stills: `?shot=descent-door`, `descent-<1..7>`.
  - Flagged for the owner: the shaft stands on level meadow ground 240–640 m out (the brief suggested "a high, quiet point"); the memories, the stranger and the book are drawn as light, not models; not yet in Tours.
- 2026-10-07: the drowned Maya city (the owner's ancient civilizations brief, SPEC 1; the telling `MAYAN`, `audio/ancient-civs/mayan.mp3`, Aria, 251.40 s decoded, 40 cues, used as delivered).
  - Where: the brief asks for the world's own underwater ruin sites, not new geography. The city takes the deepest of the nine ruin sites (`AREA_SITES` in `depths.ts`, ~26 m of water, for a pyramid ~18 m tall), in place of that site's ruin and its ring of stillness; its floor is levelled within 50 m (`levelGround` in `terrain.ts`). On the map (Deep) as "The drowned Maya city".
  - The city (`world/ancient/mayan.ts`, local frame: the stair faces the shore, the way you come):
    - the pyramid: nine sloping terraces, a band at each, the lowest buried in drifted sand; a grand stair between balustrades ending in serpent heads; a small temple on top with its doorway and roof comb;
    - the processional way: a low white causeway; beside it six stelae, each with a rounded top, a standing ruler in low relief between columns of glyph blocks (`stelaAtlas`), and a plinth. Their carvings warm with a little gold as you come within ~12 m;
    - the reading wall: a fallen lintel lying on its side, dense with glyph bands, a broken piece and a toppled jamb beside it;
    - the ball court: two long mounds with sloping benches, coursed walls and a stone ring on each, open at both ends;
    - the plaza: broken paving, five round altars (some tilted, half sunk), and the sand's ripples slowly moving over it.
    - The glyphs are decorative shapes in the manner of Maya blocks (cartouches, bars and dots, scrolls, faces, petals), never real words or names.
  - Stone: the buildings are the game's cut masonry (`seaMasonry` in `world/ancient/kit.ts` over the shared `landStone`: small courses of pale limestone, recessed joints, each stone its own tone), with silt on what faces up and the surface's light playing over it as faint caustics. Carved stones use `seaStone` (the scanned sandstone, triplanar, with the carving read from a canvas).
  - Light: soft shafts from the surface to the plaza, the pyramid and the stelae, each breathing on its own time; near the city the water itself is a little greener and its shafts stronger (`UnderwaterEffect.u.uTint/uShaft/uShaftCol`, eased in over 60 m).
  - Life: fish schools on loops round the pyramid and along the stelae (never through them), and two mantas passing far overhead (the world's own fish pack). No turtle: there is no turtle model (real models or nothing).
  - Keepers (`Keeper` in `kit.ts`, recipe `MAYA_PRIEST` in `world/figures.ts`: a feathered headdress of jade and gold, a collar, a deep green wrapped garment). They are the temple archetypes' particle figures on the wanderer's skeleton:
    - one sits still on the temple platform before its door;
    - one walks slowly along the stelae, pausing at each, facing it;
    - one kneels at an altar and rises a while on a long loop.
    - Underwater they are buoyant and slower. They gather into form within ~45 m, fade by ~110 m, turn only their head toward you within ~16 m, never move toward you, and are never solid.
  - The telling (`Ancients` in `world/ancient/index.ts`) begins once, the first time you come into the city's water (within 62 m), and plays on wherever you go: leaving never ducks, cuts or restarts it (the owner's rule for these areas). It doesn't start while "Only nature" rests the voices or over an archive narration; then it waits for your next coming in.
  - Solid: the pyramid's terraces (each up to its own height), the temple, the stelae, the lintel, the ball court's mounds and the altars.
  - Stills: `?shot=ancient-mayan-<a|b|c|d|e>` (approach, stelae, ball court, temple top, reading wall); `&warm=S` lives the world S seconds first, keeping the still's own view.
- 2026-10-07: Atlantis under the sea (the owner's ancient civilizations brief, SPEC 2; the telling `ATLANTIS`, `audio/ancient-civs/atlantis.mp3`, Aria, 269.71 s decoded, 97 cues, used as delivered).
  - Where: the next deepest of the world's ruin sites (~22 m of water; `AREA_SITES`), in place of its ruin, its floor levelled within 64 m. On the map (Deep) as "Atlantis".
  - The city (`world/ancient/atlantis.ts`), after Plato's rings: rings of land 2.3 m high, faced and paved in pale marble (the shared masonry, `seaMasonry(…, "marble")`), alternate with the canals (the dark lake floor) about a central islet; you swim, or walk, ring to ring (`standHooks` in `terrain.ts` lets you stand on the rings).
    - The acropolis: three steps up to a round platform; a ring of twelve great fluted marble columns (seven standing, five fallen outward and broken in two), lintels still spanning three pairs, great blocks fallen about, a broken pediment lying across the floor, and a throne with a high back facing the way you come.
    - The colonnade: great columns down the avenue from the outer ring toward the centre, some standing, some toppled outward in turn, their drums apart.
    - Bridges over the canals along the avenue (the inner whole, the outer broken mid-arch, its keystone fallen in the canal) and two more elsewhere.
    - The harbour beyond the outer ring: three stone quays with mooring rings, the ribs and keels of two great hulls half buried in the silt.
    - Four great dim crystals in broken stone housings, a faint light breathing in them once every ~20 s (never flashy); pottery and worked blocks scattered for close discovery; small debris tumbling slowly past; a gentle current of motes running round the canals.
  - Light: colder and deeper than the Maya city (the water bluer, fewer and dimmer shafts), and two gold shafts (on the acropolis and the avenue) that swell where the telling turns while it speaks (each cue time of `ATLANTIS`).
  - Life: fish keep to the outer rings (the centre feels emptied); one great ray (4.2 m) circles the acropolis.
  - Wanderers (recipe `ATLANTEAN`: tall, a hooded robe cut in few broad planes, pale blue, crystal-blue at collar and hem): two walk slowly round the canals, one walks the avenue, one stands still on the acropolis facing the sea; and the still presence (recipe `ATLANTEAN_PRESENCE`: larger, a long veil, a pale disc behind the head) on the acropolis's far side, a memorial more than a character. Never solid, never approaching.
  - The telling follows the Maya city's rule: once, on first coming into the city's water (within 64 m), playing on wherever you go.
  - Solid: the acropolis, its standing columns, the throne, the pediment, the avenue's standing columns, the quays and the crystals.
  - Stills: `?shot=ancient-atlantis-<a|b|c|d|e>` (the approach, the acropolis, the colonnade, the harbour, a crystal).
- 2026-10-07: Mu, the drowned land of Lemuria (the owner's ancient civilizations brief, SPEC 3; the telling `LEMURIA`, `audio/ancient-civs/lemuria.mp3`, Aria, 246.84 s decoded, 41 cues, used as delivered). Peace: the oldest and gentlest of the three, the relief after the warning.
  - Where: the ruin site nearest the shore where you wake (~18 m of water, the lightest of the three), in place of its ruin; its floor levelled within 46 m. On the map (Deep) as "Mu, the drowned land".
  - The land (`world/ancient/lemuria.ts`): nothing cut square, the stone grown smooth (the scanned sandstone, `seaStone`, with soft green growth and silt), the sea having adopted it:
    - the circle of the elders: nine tall smooth stones like river pebbles stood on end, about a shallow hollow of sand and shells whose sand turns slowly in many fine arms (the listening; `spiralSand`);
    - rounded platforms of stone you can stand on, and broad shallow garden terraces you can climb (`standHooks`), with sea-gardens on them: tapering blades in muted greens and golds swaying slowly, each on its own time;
    - coral fused with the stone: branching growths in soft ochre, rose and sand rising out of stones, platforms and mounds;
    - three star stones, each pierced near its top by a round sight-hole and notched at its crown, tipped back so the holes look up toward the light;
    - five dwelling mounds, low half-buried domes, each with a dark oval doorway set into its skin (no inside).
  - Light: the warmest of the three (the water golden-green, warm soft shafts breathing).
  - Life: dense small fish in three schools. No turtles or jellyfish: there are no models for them (real models or nothing).
  - Elders (recipe `LEMURIAN_ELDER`: shorter and rounder, a broad simple robe in earth and sea tones, a soft hood), the most still of all: one sits at the circle, one stands there and raises a hand in blessing a while on a long loop, one tends the garden terraces (kneeling, rising a while, kneeling). Heads turn to you when near; they never approach and are never solid.
  - The telling follows the same rule: once, on first coming into Mu's water (within 48 m), playing on wherever you go.
  - Solid: the circle's stones, the star stones and the mounds.
  - Stills: `?shot=ancient-lemuria-<a|b|c|d|e>` (the circle, the terraces, the star stones, a mound, the hollow).
- 2026-10-07: the pyramid's false doors, its meditation and a more realistic foot (ancient civilizations 4/4; the owner's brief; never merged here: the owner merges).
  - Eight false doors (`world/falseDoors.ts`), two on each face, 26 m either side of its middle (the north entrance between), after the Old Kingdom chapels' (the owner: the first, a deep booth, "looks off"; references checked). Each is one slab set into a low chapel wall at the pyramid's foot:
    - a torus moulding round its edge under a cavetto cornice with palm leaves; the upper lintel; the panel (the remembered one seated on a lion-legged seat before a table of tall half-loaves) between two apertures; the lower lintel; outer and inner jambs; a round drum over the narrow central slot;
    - every recess only 3–20 cm deep, never a passage;
    - the slab painted as granite (red, black flecks), the wash worn at its edges and foot.
  - The writing (the owner: "missing Egyptian writing, smaller and more detail"): real hieroglyphic sign shapes (owl, vulture, quail chick, viper, cobra, reed, water, mouth, loaf, basket, bolt, hand, arm, eye, ankh, djed, was-sceptre, sun, house, offering mat, bee, sedge, feather, seated figures, jars), ~10 cm, cut in sunk relief (a bump from the carving, so each takes its own shadow), grouped in quadrats as inscriptions are, in ruled columns down every jamb and the slot, three registers on the upper lintel and one on the lower; a standing figure (wig, collar, kilt, staff and sceptre) at the foot of each column. Their order is decorative: they spell no names or words.
  - Before each door, an offering table in the shape of the sign for an offering (a loaf on a mat), its spout toward you, loaves and a jar on it. Each door is solid (a turned box collider).
  - The stone exhales: each door breathes on its own cycle of 8–12 s at its own phase. The exhale (the first ~55%) swells a warm gold glow in the niche and breathes fine motes out and up. Within ~4 m both deepen. Within a few metres a soft breath tone is heard (`audio.stoneBreath`: a band of noise round 700 Hz on the world bus, never louder than 0.05).
  - The pyramid meditation (`PYRAMID-MEDITATION`, `audio/ancient-civs/pyramid-meditation.mp3`, 477.36 s decoded, cues embedded; the audio is used as delivered) begins once a session. It starts when you stand in the calm place on the plaza before the north face (20 m round a point 24 m out). You must be on the ground: not climbing, flying or swimming. The voices must be on and no archive narration playing. It plays on wherever you go. No figures at the pyramid.
  - The pyramid made more real (`pyramid.ts`):
    - the casing reads only the scan's grain (the block scan's own bricks fought the drawn courses);
    - the casing weathers: grime and drifted sand at the foot, streaks down the faces, broad patches worn to a rougher, browner core (more at the foot and the corners), and every block at its own slight tilt with the level joints a little recessed;
    - a paved apron of flagstones 7 m wide round the foot, its outer edge broken by loose and lifted slabs;
    - sand drifted against the faces, in the corners and between the doors on the east, south and west (you walk over the apron and the drifts: `standHooks`);
    - a soft contact shadow round the foot.
  - Stills: `?shot=pyramid-foot|pyramid-door|pyramid-near|pyramid-corner|pyramid-calm`.
  - Fixed on the way: `THREE.MathUtils.smoothstep(x, min, max)` was called with its edges reversed. This affected the doors' nearness and breath tone (every door counted as near), the drowned cities' water tint, the Maya stelae's glow (it should come only within 4–12 m) and the ancient figures' fade (now 60–110 m). Rule: for a fall-off, write `1 − smoothstep(d, near, far)`; never reverse the edges.
- 2026-10-07: Göbekli Tepe (the owner's brief; the telling `GOBEKLI`, Aria, `audio/ancient-civs/gobekli.mp3`, 331.44 s decoded, cues embedded; used as delivered; never merged here). The one ancient telling under the open sky, after the drowned cities.
  - The site (`GOBEKLI` in `terrain.ts`): the world's highest dry hill toward the golden hour (east by north-east, 1–1.9 km out), with dry ground all round, a plain below and the snowy massif beyond. Its top is levelled. Four enclosures are dug 2.5 m into it, and the unexcavated tells are raised over it (all as pads, before any ground is built). Three sister hills stand 380–700 m out on the plain, each with a pillar head on its crown. Trees and caves keep clear. On the map as "Göbekli Tepe".
  - The enclosures (`world/ancient/gobekli.ts`): rings of dry-stone wall (the masonry in small rough courses, stones along the top), D and C with a gap where a boardwalk ramp comes down.
    - Each ring has T-pillars set into the wall facing in, and two taller ones at its centre, side by side, facing the gap.
    - The pillars are figures: the T's head is the shoulders, arms are carved down the broad sides to hands meeting over the belly, with a belt and a fox pelt hanging from it.
    - Some carry animals in low relief: fox, boar, lion, snake, scorpion, birds.
    - In D, just inside its gap and turned to the ramp, is the vulture stone: the great bird with its wing out over the disc, the scorpion below, the headless man, small birds, three bags along its head.
    - Walls and pillars are solid; you enter by the ramps.
  - Around them:
    - the tells under grass, some with pillar heads breaking the turf (still: their stillness against the moving grass is the effect);
    - boardwalks on posts with rope rails: a spine across the top, ramps down into D and C, a ring round C's rim, lookouts over B and A (walked on: `standHooks`);
    - the quarry at the hill's edge: a shelf of bedrock with a pillar half cut from it, lying in its trench, the work stopped;
    - limestone breaking the turf.
  - Life:
    - dry grass over the hilltop in waves of wind (off the plain), a few wild flowers in it;
    - dust turning in the low light over the rings, and seed heads blown along the ground;
    - swifts circling high: the game's small bird model, darkened. There is no swift model (real models or nothing); the owner to judge.
  - Two builders (`GOBEKLI_BUILDER`, the temple figures' particle idiom; undyed wool, ochre, stone grey): one standing still between D's central pillars facing in with the stones, one walking C's rim pausing at its pillars. None on the mounds or at the quarry. No dialogue, no collision.
  - The telling begins once, the first time you come onto the hilltop, and plays on wherever you go.
  - Stills: `?shot=gobekli-a` … `gobekli-g` (approach, into D, D's central pair, the vulture stone, C and its rim walk, the quarry, from the air).
- 2026-10-07: Nan Madol (the owner's brief; the telling `NAN-MADOL`, Aria, `audio/ancient-civs/nan-madol.mp3`, 281.32 s decoded, cues embedded; used as delivered; never merged here). The coastal one: dawn, open sky, water to wade.
  - The site (`NAN_MADOL` in `terrain.ts`): the world's own straight shore toward the dawn (south-east, 0.9–1.9 km out), dry land behind and a great lake falling to ~14 m beyond (the open sea), clear of every home and monument, the pyramid, Göbekli Tepe and the peaks. The lagoon's floor is levelled to 0.72 m below the water (wading: the controller swims only below 1 m), a beach behind it, the reef outside the seawall falling away. Kept clear. On the map as "Nan Madol".
  - The basalt (`world/ancient/nanMadol.ts`): every wall is long hexagonal basalt columns laid log-cabin fashion, a course of stretchers along the wall then a course of headers across it with their ends standing out, all in one instanced draw over a dark core.
    - The basalt is black and wet, darker and glistening toward the waterline, with a weed line at it and pale salt on dry tops.
    - The islets are platforms of it with coral-rubble tops, two low steps up from the water at each, and walls on some.
  - The islets:
    - the mortuary (after Nandauwas): the tallest walls (6.2 m), their corners swept up, a narrow gate, an inner court within a second wall, and a tomb of logs roofed with logs, with offering stones before the gate;
    - a walled islet;
    - a courtyard with a tidal pool holding the sky;
    - house platforms;
    - the canoe landing with mooring stones;
    - two small islets of palms.
    - All are walked on; their walls are solid.
  - The seawall along the open side, 3.2 m, the surf breaking white against its outer face in sets running along it, and heard: a soft surf bed under everything within ~300 m (`audio.surf`, a band of noise in slow swells, above 200 Hz).
  - Life:
    - palms leaning over the canals (the world's own, `wilds.ts` `palmGeometry`/`palmMaterial`, now exported);
    - mangroves gripping the walls' feet, on prop roots arching into the water, with round crowns;
    - fish schools in the canals (`Swimmers`, which now take a shallow `top` and `bob`). No turtle: there is no model (real models or nothing).
  - The tide: faint streaks on the canals drift one way and then the other, turning every ~3 minutes. Mist lies on the water and thins over the first four minutes of a visit.
  - Two figures (`NAN_MADOL_WATCHER`, the temple figures' particle idiom; bark cloth in sea and basalt tones, a shell collar): the watcher on the seawall facing the open water, and the keeper in the mortuary's inner court. No dialogue, no collision.
  - The telling begins once, the first time you come into the lagoon, and plays on wherever you go.
  - Stills: `?shot=nanmadol-a` … `nanmadol-f` (from the shore, the western canal, the mortuary's gate, its inner court, the seawall, from the air).
- 2026-10-08: Göbekli Tepe as it stood when it was built (the owner: "like it was when it was built, not a museum with a bridge… realistic"; supersedes the boardwalks, the tells and the pillar heads breaking the turf in the Göbekli entry above).
  - Gone: the boardwalks, rope rails, ramps and lookouts; the unexcavated tells (`GOBEKLI_PLAN.mounds` and their raised pads).
  - The enclosures stay sunk into the hill (as built, semi-subterranean). Into D and C a stair of ten stone steps goes down the bank through the gap (`GOBEKLI_PLAN.stair`: from the hilltop's edge to the floor, walkable through `standHooks`). A stone bench of fitted blocks under slabs runs round the inside of every wall between the pillars (you can sit on it).
  - The pillars are freshly cut: `landStone(…, "fresh")`, a new finish with no lichen and no rain streaks, used for them and for the houses' plaster.
  - The builders' houses on the open hilltop (`GOBEKLI_PLAN.houses`, four): rectangular dry-stone walls plastered inside, a door with a lintel and threshold, a flat roof of timber beams (their ends showing) under packed earth. Before each, a hearth of ringed stones with embers breathing under ash, a grinding slab with its handstone, a stone bowl. The walls are solid.
  - A cistern cut into the bedrock holding still dark water (`GOBEKLI_PLAN.cistern`, a pad sinks the ground under it).
  - The sister hills each carry a pair of whole standing pillars, no longer buried heads.
  - Three builders now: the third kneels at the first house's grinding slab. The rim walker walks the ground.
  - Stills: `?shot=gobekli-h` (a house, its hearth and slab), `gobekli-i` (the stair into C, from its floor); `b` is now "down into D from the stair's head".

- 2026-10-08: "Start the tour" near every tour place, natural angles when the view is steered, and the desert's walk (the owner, iPhone screenshot of the desert lesson looking up the streetlight poles: "wrong angle"; "add start tour in a big enough radius within the entrance to those areas").
  - Start the tour (`#tour-start`, `tourOfferFrame` in main.ts, `TOUR_PLACES`): within 45 m of the entrance of the densities, the school of the adept, past choices, the world after the veil, the long descent, the temple and the pyramid (for the Duat), or inside its start while no tour runs (a monument's lobby, the area, the temple hall, the pyramid's rooms or the Duat), a button offers its tour. From outside it walks you to the door and in (the monuments and the temple through their walk-throughs; the areas walk you to the door, and going in begins the guide); inside, it begins at once. Arriving through a door still begins the tour by itself; the button is for coming near, arriving another way, and after a tour was ended (✕). The stick or the button takes over on the way.
  - A view steered for you (the gravity point, contemplation's gaze) keeps to a natural pitch, 17° below to 13° above level (`FollowCamera`, after everything else): never craning up a pole or into the sky, never staring at the ground. The hand's own look is never limited. Contemplation's third gaze in the open world is the sky low over the land, no longer overhead.
  - The desert lesson's focus is at a walker's height (`centreY` 1.8, was the default 6 m): the view rests on the road, the walker, the lantern and the footprints.
  - The desert's walk reads as a walk: the walker keeps their place before you, as before, but now the streetlights (poles, lights and glows) pass by and fall behind, and forty stones along the verge come out of the dark and go by (they read as walking on the spot while only the road's grain moved).

While it is open the game's controls, hints, whispers and player rest (`body.mapping`), so nothing shows through it, and its backdrop is near opaque. On the map, the places that aren't archetypes are small points in their kind's colour in a fine ring (monuments gold, ancient worlds sand, lessons rose, the deep teal), not all the shore's spiral; only the shore keeps it. No name is written across a mark (marks reserve their room; the chosen place's name always shows). - 2026-10-09: film mode, the game records its own tour (the owner's build prompt; `docs/film-mode.md`, `src/debug/film.ts`, `src/debug/filmClock.ts`). `?film=<tour>` (or `grand`, the whole walk) wakes at the shore, plays the tour with every control and word hidden (`body.film`), and offers `inward-journey-<tour>.webm` at the end with its size; `&fps`, `&scale`, `&until`, `&seed`, `&fast=1`. Loaded only with `?film`: with it absent nothing runs (the clock module checks the address and returns; `film.ts` is its own chunk).
  - Real time: a composite canvas (the game's canvas plus the dark or white of each crossing's fade, a DOM layer a canvas capture would miss) through `captureStream` and `MediaRecorder`; the audio is the engine's whole master bus tapped after its compressor (`AudioEngine.tapStream`), so voice, bed, tones and one-shots are all in. The best graphics tier is held. One "Begin filming" click only if the browser won't start sound by itself.
  - Fast: time is virtual (`performance.now`, `Date.now`, timers and `requestAnimationFrame` on a clock the film steps 1/fps at a time), every frame is encoded with WebCodecs into a WebM (`webm-muxer`, MIT, only in the film chunk). The sound is rebuilt from a timeline of what the engine played (`Tape` in `core/audio.ts`: every voice start and stop with its fade, the water bed and its ducking, the master level), mixed in 10 s chunks. The generated layer (pad, bowls, chimes, one-shots) is random and live and is NOT in a fast capture; say so. `<tour>.timeline.json` lists every event with its frame. No WebCodecs: a zip of frames, the timeline and an ffmpeg script.
  - The game's own clocks changed only where needed: `AudioEngine.now()` (the virtual clock in fast mode, whose context is an offline one that is never rendered), `Narration` reads it and ends a voice by the clock in fast mode; `Math.random` is seeded in any film mode; the device's save is neither read nor written. The tours' camera was already critically damped, so no extra smoothing.
- 2026-10-09: film mode, chapters and "everything" (the owner: "extract the when and where so the TV app jumps to the right part… a get-it-all-done mode so I can walk away"; `docs/film-mode.md`). Every recording now writes `<id>.chapters.json`: the parts of the clip (one per tour stop, walk between places, Duat story, or the whole of autofly, auto-walk, genesis) with start and end seconds from the clip's first frame, a kind, and the game's own place name. They come from the tour bar's title (`tourBar().info()`, so every guided walk is covered with no per-tour code) and `devPlace()` (`FilmHost.chapter`). `?film=everything` films each tour plus autofly, auto-walk and genesis as its own clip (`&only`, `&skip`, `&grand=1`, `&flysecs`, `&walksecs`, `&fresh`), one click to pick a folder (File System Access, straight to disk) or plain downloads, then unattended: between clips `FilmHost.reset` puts every tour and mode away and returns to the shore; `manifest.json` is rewritten after each clip; finished clips are kept on the device so a restarted page resumes. A tour clip that lasts under 10 s gets a `warning` in the manifest.

The overlay (`#breath-cue`) is the lungs and one word: Breathe in, Hold, Release, Wait (the fourth side, the hold after releasing, is "Wait"). The seconds' count and its heartbeat are gone (`.count`, `breath-beat`), and the first-time line names the four sides without digits. Supersedes the counter in the 2026-10-08 square-breathing entry and the heartbeat count in the calmer-breathing entry; the on/off rhythm (three rounds shown, four resting) stays. The ⋮ How to play text still says the pattern is four seconds a side.
- 2026-10-09: film mode's waking card shows progress (the owner: "it says waking the world but I don't see anything going on"). The card was static for the whole boot (the game's own loading screen is hidden in film mode). It now names what is being waited for (`FilmHost.waking`: preparing the light (the shaders), raising the water, arriving), counts the seconds, pulses a ring, and says so if the tab is in the background (the game only wakes while in view) or if it takes over 90 s (the first load fetches the world).
- 2026-10-09: film mode no longer chokes a laptop (the owner: "buggy… gets stuck like nothing is moving in the scene"). Likely cause: real-time film mode pinned the top quality level at the screen's full pixel size and recorded that size, which a laptop cannot draw and VP9-encode in real time (a frame every few seconds). Now: the film is at most `&res=` high (default 1080, times `&scale=` if no `&res`; `Picture` shrinks the canvas into it); the game renders about that many pixels (`FilmHost.pin`: `quality.scale`); real-time capture lets the game's own adaptive quality run (only fast capture, whose time is virtual, holds the best level); the waking card and the REC dot show the live frame rate (`FilmHost.frames`) and say so when it is under 8 fps or when no frames are drawn for 10 s. If it still sticks, the card's line names the stage.
- 2026-10-09: film mode lighter while it records (the owner: "once it gets going, the scenes are stuck… or choppy"). Real-time capture now prefers H.264 (an .mp4, encoded by the computer's own video hardware; seekable) over software VP9/WebM, which at 1080p took enough CPU to slow the game; if only software is available the card's note says so. The game's canvas is copied into the film at the film's frame rate (30), not at every game frame (60), and the on-screen status updates twice a second, not every frame. Headless Chromium has no H.264, so only the VP9 path was exercised; the H.264 path is the owner's to confirm on a real machine.
- 2026-10-09: film mode in 4K (the owner: "Can't we have 4k?"). `&res=` takes names (`4k`, `1440p`, `1080p`, `720p`) or a height; the game renders at that height from any window size (`FilmHost.pin` raises the render scale above 1× to reach it), H.264/VP9 are asked at level 5.1 above 1100 px. Recommended: `?film=<tour>&res=4k&fast=1` (fast capture renders at its own pace; its sound is the voice and water bed only). Live 4K needs a strong GPU and a hardware encoder. See `docs/film-mode.md`.
- 2026-10-09: the film setup page (the owner: "add a config page where I choose what I'll record and what level"). `public/film.html`, a plain page that does not load the game: checkboxes for every tour, autofly, auto-walk, genesis and the grand tour, quality (720p/1080p/1440p/4K), fast or live, frame rate; Start builds the `?film=` address (one clip: `film=<id>`; several: `film=everything&only=…`). Choices are remembered on the device. The tour list is written in the page: add a tour to `WALKS` in main.ts and to `CLIPS` there.

- 2026-10-13: the mobile performance plan (`PERF-01`–`10`; the owner's launch plan). Every change pixel-identical in `?shot=` stills under `?webgl` (meadow, a massif close and from above, rooms 1 and the call), or left out. The plan's line numbers were stale (the files had grown); the code it described was found by content.
  - Done: a steady 30 fps (Low Power Mode, or iOS holding a hot phone) never climbs quality back up, it only holds (`quality.ts`); the unused lake-floor caustics removed from the ground shader; the snow's three noises read only on the mountains (the crags already were); the water's ripple loop runs only while a ripple lives (`uRippleActive`); the god rays' two passes skipped while unseen (`rayPasses`, `autoUpdate`); motes not rebuilt when only the render scale changes; the terrain fill asks about finer tiles by integer key with no strings or arrays per vertex; no per-frame vectors for walk targets, the gravity point or contemplation's gaze (`gpOut`, `gazeV`); the far tiles read heights through the coarse cache (`corner`, exact on its lattice); the tap's raycaster reused.
  - Left out, with cause: sharing the ground shader's `setup()` between colour and relief (its shared vars broke the scans' texturing: the sand went flat and dark); the star's shadow off in places apart (the rooms are lit by the star and its shadows show there: Room 1's towers, the call's causeway); memoizing `rawHeight` at boot (53,228 calls, 53,133 distinct points: nothing to reuse; the module's import is ~270 ms in Node); the coarse march in tap-to-ground and `nearWater` (one call a tap or a frame, and exactness at the edges would change).

- 2026-10-13: the horses are spirits of light (the owner: the glass horses were "super lame"; "use something like the particles to make them appear and go away, almost like a spirit"). Supersedes the horses in the animals entries above; the birds are unchanged.
  - Each horse is a body of motes (`world/spiritHorses.ts`) on the surface of the real horse model (1,400 a horse on a phone, 2,200 on desktop), moved by its own recorded gallop. The model's morph targets are read on the CPU; the mesh is never drawn.
  - A horse appears as motes gathering out of the air into its form (~3.6 s), 16–44 m from you, or beside one already there. It roams in a slow dreamlike gait for 30–75 s, a few wisps of light lifting off its back and trailing behind. Then it loosens into rising, swirling motes and is gone. It appears again elsewhere 8–30 s later.
  - Rushing at one makes it dissolve quickly. In stillness, those near come close and stand, and stay while the stillness lasts.
  - None come while you are in the water or in a place apart; any that are there go.
  - The cast is pale moonlight with pearl, one in three warmer gold. It uses the figures' soft point and its light is contained.
  - Scale is taken from the rest shape: the geometry's bounding box takes in every frame of the gallop, so it made a 0.3 m horse. The hooves are kept on the ground at every moment of the gallop.
  - `herdOf` stays for the rooms (Room 2's herd).
  - Still: `?shot=horses&warm=7` (three summoned across the view; `warm=2` shows them gathering).
- 2026-10-13: rabbits and squirrels, spirits of light like the horses (the owner: "also fluid and beautiful squirrels, rabbits").
  - No CC0 animated rabbit or squirrel model could be found (Quaternius, Kenney, OpenGameArt; the paid packs aren't CC0). So, unlike the house rule's "real animated models", these are drawn as motes on forms of soft rounded volumes (`world/spiritCritters.ts`): body, haunches, head, ears, paws, and for the squirrel a tail of six overlapping fluffy puffs. Each mote lies on the outer skin (points inside another volume are dropped), so the animal reads as one round, furred outline, never a stiff shape. A bought model can replace either form later.
  - Gaits written as curves of time. The rabbit hops (haunches driving, the body arching and pitching, ears laid back in the air) and sits between hops, nose twitching, ears turning, now and then up on its haunches to look about. The squirrel darts in quick bounds, its tail streaming flat and rippling, then sits up with its paws to its chest and its tail standing behind, flicking.
  - Three of each (900 motes an animal on a phone, 1,300 on desktop). Rabbits appear in open ground 7–24 m from you; squirrels at the foot of nearby trees (`creation.touchables()`) and dart from tree to tree.
  - Like the horses: they gather out of motes (~2.6 s), live 40–90 s, loosen into rising motes, and come again elsewhere. Rushing at one sends it bounding off as it fades. In stillness they come within 2–3 m and sit facing you, and stay while the stillness lasts. None come in the water or a place apart.

- 2026-10-14: new versions arrive without deleting web data (the owner: "content is not refreshed unless web data is deleted, force refresh upon booting"). The service worker answered the page itself from its precache for good, and the boot's freshness check (`core/fresh.ts`) went through that same worker, so it always saw the old build; a new version arrived only after the web data was cleared, or long after.
  - The page and the small unhashed files beside it (quality.json, breathing.json, the manifest) now come from the network first (`public/sw.js`, revalidating the browser's cache). The kept copy is used only offline, or after 4 s with no answer. Hashed scripts and styles stay cache-first: their names change with every build.
  - The freshness check asks with `?fresh=`, which the worker lets through, so it hears the server's word.
  - At every boot and every return to the game the worker is asked to look for a newer version of itself (`registerSW` `onRegisteredSW` → `update()`). If a new one takes over before the journey begins, the page loads again on it, once (`controllerchange`).
  - The old "New content is available" confirm() is gone.
  - Tested headless with the real builds. After a newer deploy, the next launch runs it, and it still opens offline. A phone still holding the previous worker gets the new one on its first launch after this ships and runs it from the launch after.

Supersedes the Piper voicing in the temple-rites and maze entries. The opening's Piper lines were already unused.

    - Each is turned by its own door and walk-in heading so its door faces the maze, its front on the forecourt's edge (`FRONT_R`).

- 2026-10-15: ⋮ → "Load the newest version" (the owner: "it really sticks to whatever is the prior version"). The automatic refresh of 2026-10-14 needs the phone to have taken the new service worker first, and a Home Screen game that is only ever resumed can sit on the old one. The button saves your place, unregisters the service worker, deletes its cached copy of the game (every cache but `inward-journey-assets`, so offline downloads stay), and reloads at a new `?v=` address that no cache can answer. Hovering it shows this version's build time. When the game sees a newer build mid-journey, its whisper now points to the button. Tested headless: the precache went, the asset cache and its contents stayed, and the page reloaded at `?v=`.

- 2026-10-15: the maze's chase camera (the owner: "def not enjoyable… should be third person, just that the camera is always centred… research a bit"). Research (game camera practice: John Nesky's "50 Game Camera Mistakes", rolling-ball and racing follow cameras, Cinemachine's third-person follow): keep the hard rules (never inside a wall, the player always in sight) and the soft ones (angle, distance) apart; follow where the player really goes, not the stick or the level; turn with a damped spring, frame-rate independent; don't solve walls by zooming in and out (that is the bouncing). Supersedes the corridor rail and ring arc for the maze.
  - `FollowCamera.chase` (`player/camera.ts`): the view stands on the ball's own path ~3 m back (recorded every 0.25 m), so it is always behind the way the ball truly goes, the ball held at the centre of the screen, and, the ball having just been there, inside the corridor with nothing between (the clearance rarely has to pull in). Turning back on its path begins the path afresh. The turn is a critically damped spring (ω 3.2: a soft start, no overshoot). Pitch settles to ~21°. A look-drag still works; the view comes back behind ~1 s after it. Measured on the real way in (route 0, 20 s): the ball centred, 33 m walked, the view ~3.7 m behind, a wall between it and the ball in 5% of samples.
- 2026-10-15: the maze's chase view rises instead of zooming (the owner: "slightly better but it should not zoom in that much bc then you get blinded"). Where a wall comes between the ball and the view, the view no longer comes in close: it rises and looks down over the ball (`FollowCamera.crane`, up to ~77°, eased up quickly and down slowly), its foot pulled in only as far as the clear reach of the corridor, so the ball stays centred and small; it never comes nearer than 2.6 m (`chaseMin`). It also swings round to the nearest clear side (offsets up to ±1.1 rad, tested 15 times a second). The maze's camera ceiling is 4.2 m over the floor (was 3.2), still under the walls' tops (4.6 m), so nothing beyond them shows. Measured (20 s walks): route 0 the view ~3.7 m off, a wall between it and the ball 4.4% of samples; route 3 (sharp door turns) 13.3% (12.7% with the zoom; 31% without zoom or crane).
- 2026-10-15: the maze's chase view foresees walls (the owner: "research online or GitHub best practices for these kinds of scene"). Research: Haigh-Hutchinson's Real-Time Cameras (navigation and occlusion: predictive cameras navigate confined spaces better than reactive ones; damped springs over hard limits; paths from the player's own movement history), Unity Cinemachine's Deoccluder (pull forward vs preserve height or distance, damping, minimum occlusion time), the gamedev "whiskers" practice (detect early, then fade, pull in or swing round; let thin obstacles break the line), and open three.js controllers (yomotsu/camera-controls, three-player-controller with three-mesh-bvh). Applied: the chase view also tests where the ball will be in half a second, the view held in the same place relative to it, and rises for the worse of the two (`FollowCamera` clearance block, `predA`/`predB`). Tried and left out: ignoring occlusions shorter than 0.2 s (route 3 went from 13.3% to 18.8%). Measured (20 s walks): a wall between the view and the ball route 0 2.8% (was 4.4%), route 3 8.3% (was 13.3%); the view ~3.7–3.8 m off.

A touch that begins on the terrace draws; one that begins on a form shapes it; one that begins anywhere else looks about, as ever (`Input.capture`); a tap off the terrace closes building. The stick still takes the lower left.
  - Drawing: the stroke is a faint trail of light on the stone as it's drawn; let go, it's read (`readStroke`: $1's resampling, then straightness, closure, total turning, how evenly it turns, whether it winds in): an open stroke → a low wall along it (1.3 m, battered, rounded top, sunk into the earth, rounded ends); a closed round loop → a ring wall (≥ 2.4 m across, `RING_D`: a ring you could stand in) or a pillar (smaller: one you could put your arms round); a closed loop with corners (a square, a triangle) → a wall round its own shape; a spiral (> 1¼ turns, winding in or out) → a fluted spire, 3–9 m by how wide it was drawn; a touch → an orb of light at rest height. A scribble sinks back without a word. Tested on 14 strokes, wobbly ones included.
  - Shaping: a tap chooses a form (a soft glow over it, a faint ring at its base); a drag moves it (it follows the finger, eased: weighted, unhurried); a second finger drawn up or down lifts or lowers it (orbs higher), the two turning turn it; a tap on the chosen form, or the small dashed ◌ by it, lets it go: it sinks back into the earth and its essence returns. No scaling.
  - Essence: stone from rock meditations (each completed one, a measure), gold from heart notes the person writes (not the oracle's, not brought-in memories; each a measure). Two round glyphs hold it as light, fuller as it gathers, never a number; a tap picks the ink (the last used is kept). Cost by size: a wall 0.08 a metre, a ring by its circumference, a pillar 0.25, a spire 0.25 + 0.06 a metre of height, an orb 0.05; not enough and the stroke fades unforged. A gold form keeps the newest note at the moment it was drawn; tapped outside building, that note opens in the reader (if the note was since deleted, a whisper: "This held a memory you let go. Its shape stays.").
  - Growth on the breath (the square breathing's phases, `breathing.json`): a wall rises out of the earth over one exhale (4 s, eased), a ring or pillar blooms over one inhale, an orb kindles over one, a spire climbs on each inhale and exhale and rests through the holds (three climbs, ~20 s); a seam of light runs round its foot while it moves through the earth, brightest mid-way. Forms are solid (the shared stone, and gold, are solid materials); orbs are not.

Downloading a recording takes real time while the virtual clock runs on, so the simulation behaves like a phone on a slow connection. What was found and fixed:

The oracle's sun disposed its sprite's geometry as it faded (as did a lesson's or the Duat's `VisionStage` and a planted tree growing a stage), so the shared quad's GPU buffers went for every sprite still drawn, and Safari's WebGPU refused the next draw. The forest path of the world after the veil opens into the clearing, and there stands a court of honey sandstone in ruins: a hall's front (two storeys, arched doors, windows) with its returns, an arcade of five arches closing the far side, low garden walls round a carved basin on a round plinth. Their lower courses stand, the rest lies in the grass. As beat 3 is spoken (89.7 s, its own progress) the court is made new: a scaffold of light draws the finished walls and arches in thin gold; stone by stone, bottom up, each block lifts from where it lay, glowing beneath, turns and settles into its course; the basin rises from the turf; the scaffold fades as the walls stand; the lawn greens, the clipped shrubs swell and open white blossoms, flowers open across the grass, wisteria lengthens down from the hall and the arcade in white and lavender; the clearing's haze thins; last a wave of warm light passes through every stone. Once told it stays made new. Walls (between their doors), the basin are solid; shrubs keep off the way. The view holds the court's heart while it is told. The clearing's threads of light stay low and clear of the court. Beat 4's newcomers no longer sit about (the owner: "random sitters"): they stand braced at the treeline, and once their line is spoken their armour's shards fall away and fade. The sky over the vale is the battle: a vortex of painted cloud (two discs in its plane, a cloud shader: swirled arms, inner bands turning faster, the nearer layer darker and slower), gold-lit toward its eye with gold linings where the arms thin, slate and charcoal to its edges; the eye a hole the light pours through (painted into the far disc: a separate sprite for it cut a square out of the clouds); six shafts of light from the eye onto the vale; two hosts in flight round it, each figure one soft sprite drawn as a winged form of light (a slender bright body, two wings beating, turned along its flight; never strings of points), the guardians in gold one way, the shadowed ones in silver-blue the other; ribbons of intention arcing between their sides in the vortex's own plane, pulses running along them. It follows beat 5's progress: the vortex gathers and the eye opens, the gold host comes with the guardians, the shadow host with the shadowed ones, the ribbons at "the arms of thought", and as the telling ends the hosts slow, the clouds part and the eye softens; once told it stays passed. The guardians' columns of warm light stay on the ground; the tall husks with their spirals ("alien head-beams") are gone. The view looks up into it while it is told. Still frames: `?shot=veil-5&war=0…1`.
- 2026-10-07: Phase 3, S1, the post-processing finish (`gpu/post.ts`; the master prompt's global beautify, systems not scenes). Before/after sheets of a 39-still sweep: `docs/sweeps/s1-post/`.
  - Clarity: before tone mapping, each pixel's light against a wide blur of itself (`gaussianBlur`, a quarter of the resolution), drawn out in ratio (`CLARITY` 0.32, clamped 0.72–1.6), never in the deepest dark. Forms stand out of the haze (the pyramid, the meadow's sky and aurora, scanned ground, planks) without darkening the night.
  - The grade's contrast is a soft S about its pivot (power curves meeting with the same slope), so 0 stays 0 and 1 stays 1: the old linear contrast crushed the night's darkest values and clipped highlights.
  - Vibrance (`VIBRANCE` 0.3): colour drawn out of muted things (stone, soil, leaves), not out of what is already vivid (glows, crystals), and not in the dark.
  - After SMAA, a sharpen limited to each pixel's four neighbours (`SHARPEN` 0.55): crisp texture, no halos. Only with SMAA (the default).
  - The vignette falls slowly from the middle to the corners (13% there), following the frame; a half-step dither ends banding in the night's long gradients.
  - Cost: one quarter-resolution blur and four more reads in the final pass.

Rest still before it and the twenty light one by one (~1.1 s each). `Area.secret`.

The moai are one carved surface each (`moaiGeometry`: superellipse rings, the features swelling out of and cut into the stone). Windswept grass (Göbekli's `dryGrass`, now shared, darker green for the night); the surf heard below the cliff. The telling begins on first coming onto the headland, plays on.
