- 2026-10-08: merging (the owner, asked to "always merge everything"; the agent proposed a narrower rule and the owner said yes). Every game PR the agent opens is merged once its checks pass, without asking. The owner's `narration/*` audio PRs are still the owner's to merge; the agent never merges them. Supersedes "the game's own PRs are merged as they go" only where it conflicted with this.
- 2026-10-08: standing working behaviours (the owner's "Universal agent behaviors", applied). (1) Reflect: a correction is proposed as a permanent rule in one line, and saved only once the owner approves it; never a rule that weakens a standing rule without flagging the conflict. (2) Narrate: a one-line status on multi-step work (stage, what is happening, rough cost); say when subagents are spawned or the model is switched. (3) Surface: anything that matters (cost, destructive or risky commands, uncertain decisions, a cheaper or better option) is said first, in plain language. (4) Changelog: each finished unit ends with files changed, what was verified and how, cost, and what is next or blocked. The paragraph of hard limits is not recorded as written: where it says never merge, the owner's later instruction ("always merge") applies, so game PRs are merged once checks pass, as in the merge rule above. Deploys and deletes still need the owner's approval.
- 2026-10-07: dev feedback mode (the owner's build prompt): `?dev=1` turns it on and the device remembers it (`?dev=0` off; `debug/devflag.ts`). With it off nothing of it loads (`debug/feedback.ts` is its own chunk, imported only in dev mode).
  - A small ✎ button sits above the round button at 40% (it hides in record mode); on a keyboard, the backtick. It takes the picture first: one fresh frame drawn and copied from the canvas at once (no preserved buffer needed), so the controls and the panel are never in it.
  - While the panel is open the game holds: the frame stops, the voice pauses (the shared pause), a tour stands still, the archive pauses; keys and touches stay in the panel (typing H never wakes genesis). Discard or Send, and it goes on from the same moment.
  - The panel, in the game's own look: the picture, "What do you see?", and chips of what it was: the still's address when there is one, the place (a monument's room by title and id, the temple and its nearest shrine, the pyramid or the Duat, the deep archive, or in the world the nearest place on the map and how far), camera position and heading, the wanderer, the tour and its stop, the voice and its time, the archive, the sky's moods, renderer and tier, the build (`__SHA__` from CI or git, and its time), the time.
  - Send downloads `<time>-feedback.png` and `<time>-feedback.md` (the words, the chips, the picture by its file name) and copies the markdown to the clipboard. Nothing is uploaded.
- 2026-10-08: the stone is solid, everywhere (the owner: "most of what goes underwater you just go through the building… every building somehow more solid… at a root level… so this doesn't happen again"). Before, only hand-listed colliders held the wanderer, so any building nobody had listed was paper, and the orb under the water ignored even those.
  - Solidity is a property of the material (`world/solidity.ts`, `solid(m)`): the shared masonry `landStone` (every monument, room, home, gate, the Duat, the ancient cities' `seaMasonry`), and the stone made apart from it (`ruinStone`, `seaStone`, the pyramid's `limestone` and `granite`, Nan Madol's basalt and rubble, the false doors' slab). Any mesh drawn in such a material, now or later, holds the wanderer by its real shape; nothing to list. `passable(object)` opts a mesh or group out. Rule: new stone for a building goes through `landStone` or calls `solid()`.
  - How: each such mesh near you (within its bounds + 14 m, found twice a second) gets a bounding volume tree once (three-mesh-bvh, MIT, `indirect` so its buffers are never touched; one built a frame). Walking (and the seabed): a capsule from a step (0.5 m) above the feet to the head is pushed sideways out of upright faces only. Floors: the highest walkable face (gentler than ~57°) up to a step above the feet is ground (stairs, platforms, fallen blocks, roofs), as well as the land. The orb and the flier: a ball (0.5 m, 1 m up) pushed out of every face from any side; under the water the orb slides along a wall instead of stopping dead. Faces are one-sided unless the material draws both, so a body that finds itself inside a closed block is let out, never trapped. Instances hidden by a zero scale are skipped.
  - Left as it was: the temple (already fully collided by hand, and its door walks through its wall), rocks and crystals (their colliders and the laying-on of hands), trees, seats, figures.
  - Measured headless: the orb swimming at Atlantis's heart now stops at the acropolis (it used to pass through to 1.7 m of the centre); walking at the school of the adept from behind stops at its walls (it used to reach the middle); the Maya city's nearest wall holds the orb 0.5 m off. No measurable cost in the frame's update time. The density, adept and past-choices walk-throughs still cross every room at its door.
- 2026-09-25: the wanderer must read as fluid, with no visible joints.
  - The skeleton (recorded animation from the CC0 Universal Animation Library) drives a ray-marched smooth union of capsules (`src/player/fluidBody.ts`).
  - The mannequin mesh is never drawn.

Defaults Claude proposed on 2026-09-24 (Samuel said "go"):
- **Art direction:**
  - the brief's world wins: the lake hub; Mind as forests, libraries, mirrors in silver-blue; Body as mountains, waterfalls, terraces in amber, green and earth red; Spirit as crystal and starlight in violet, gold and indigo.
  - Egyptian and pre-Columbian influence only as abstract linework, never temples, deities or religious symbols.
  - The older temple-based art direction from the first prototype is retired.
- **Visual DNA:**
  - Samuel's drawings. Fine glowing linework, sacred geometry, hand-drawn wobble.
  - In-engine this becomes etched-light contour lines on terrain, line mandalas, and light footprints.
- **Target:** iPhone Safari first, laptop second.
- **Narration:** new 30–90 s station meditations will be written for Samuel's approval, then voiced with his `tts` CLI as before.
  - The 21 one-line distillations in the theory file stay as source material.
  - Never quote Ra verbatim in-game.
  - Map every station to its theory passage in `content-notes.md`.
- **Engine:** Vite + TypeScript + Three.js + `postprocessing`. No runtime network calls; system fonts only.

## Source material (authoritative)
- `reference/theory/Mind__Body__Spirit___Foundational_Theory.md` is canon for meaning. It holds verbatim Ra/Q'uo quotes per archetype. Never invent quotes, doctrine or session numbers.
- Archive gaps: the Significator of the Body (12) and the Great Way of the Spirit (21) have no Ra definition. Mark them as open or unfinished (dashed or incomplete forms).
- Ra's teaching order (88.24): 1, 8, 15, 2, 9, 16, 3, 10, 17, 4, 11, 18, 5, 12, 19, 6, 13, 20, 7, 14, 21. The seven synthesis moments follow this grouping.
- `reference/RA_EVOLUTION_HANDBOOK.md` (added 2026-09-25) is Samuel's game bible:
  - cosmology, densities, the 22 archetypes card by card (Part XII);
  - dialogue seeds (XIII), a bestiary of beings (XIV), design notes (XV).
  - It is paraphrase with archive citations; verify against the theory file before quoting anything.
  - Use it to enrich content and creatures. Samuel's current focus is visuals.
- The Ra tarot deck: `reference/IMG_0033–0056.jpeg`. The overview chart: `reference/BE9C2465-….png`.
- `reference/drawings/samuel-art/` and `reference/drawings/egyptian-tarot-cards/` hold references. The brief's 19 ink drawings (2014–2016) and finished narration audio live in Samuel's `inward-journey` repo, which is not yet attached.
- `prototype/` holds earlier single-file prototypes. Behaviour ideas only; do not port their renderer.

## Audio
- The bed is `water-bed.mp3` (from the package's `water-bed.wav`), looped with a crossfade, plus soft generated tones.
- Narration ducks the bed (never mutes it). Leaving a narration fades it, never cuts it.
- No subtitles (Samuel, 2026-09-26: "remove subtitles").
- iOS 17+: set `navigator.audioSession.type = "playback"`, so sound plays with the silent switch on.
- Start audio inside the first tap ("Touch the water to begin").
- Keep music above about 200 Hz; phone speakers lose the low end.
- Narration voices: Aria is `avocado_v2:MAI_01` (warm female), Rowan is `avocado_v2:miles` (soothing male), speed 92.

## Quality bar
- Lighting is the main character: dawn light, fog with depth, water that reflects the sky, bloom used with restraint.
- **Performance:** 60 fps on a recent iPhone and at least 30 fps on mid-range devices. Adaptive quality covers pixel ratio, shadows and bloom (`src/core/quality.ts`).
- **Accessibility:**
  - keyboard, mouse and touch all work;
  - reduced motion, both automatic and as a toggle;
  - subtitles;
  - never rely on colour alone;
  - no tracking and no personal data.

## Build order
Follow the package milestones (M1–M5 above). Stop and show Samuel after each.

Ask Samuel before large aesthetic decisions. The transcripts stay the authority on meaning.
